ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u6	Mr. Marshal! Mrs. Member! Gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! The Ministry of Development, of course, has detailed data on European spending as part of the implementation of projects co-financed by European Union funds. A system of reporting and monitoring has been put in place to monitor the financial progress, as well as all of them. in-kind effects in all programmes implemented in this financial perspective. The main database is the SL2014 IT system in which data are aggregated into categories such as:: calls, applications for funding, advances and applications for payment, checks and certification of expenditure to the European Commission. The managing authorities of regional operational programmes shall also have similar systems of a local nature. The cooperation between local systems and the central system of SL2014 therefore makes it possible to provide the most accurate and up-to-date data relating to the state of implementation of both national and regional programmes. To answer the next question, I would like to stress that by 15 September 2016 16 regional programmes launched 1114 calls for nearly PLN 46 billion - EU funding - which represents almost 34% allocation for the years 2014 to 2020 The competitions announced are mostly dedicated to activities financed by the European Regional Development Fund, about 70% Ww. amounts, while for the thematic objectives financed by the European Social Fund, the value of calls currently amounts to around 30%. More than 17,6 thousand have been made available in all the calls run by 16 regional programme managing bodies. applications for funding. Their value is about PLN 45.7 billion, including funding from the European Union is about PLN 30.5 billion. This amount amounts to around 22,5% allocation for 16 regional operational programmes. Prominent leaders are programmes of Podkarpackie and Pomeranian voivodships, in which the value of applications for funding after formal evaluation exceeds 40% allocation for a given programme. To date, over 3.2 thousand have been signed. projects with a value of more than PLN 11 billion, including European Union funding of about PLN 8.5 billion. Approximately 6.2% were distributed as a result. the allocation available. In terms of the level of contracting, i.e. the allocation allocated on the basis of contracts, of course, two voivodships are such leaders: Pomeranian and Wielkopolska, where the use of the allocation is 17% respectively. and 15% . In terms of the contracted amount, the two above mentioned amounts. voivodships are equal to the Śląskie voivodship, and due to the high allocation of the value of contracts signed so far in this region, this represents around 7% resources available. The value of documented expenditure of beneficiaries amounts to over PLN 1 billion, including the European Union funds transferred to beneficiaries on the basis of payment claims submitted is nearly PLN 877 million, which represents 0.6% allocation. In the whole Warmia-Mazurskie voivodship 766 applications for funding of PLN 1.3 billion were registered, including EU funding of PLN 992 million. This represents 13% allocation and location of warmińsko-mazurskie voivodship currently on 12th place in all regional operational programmes. However, the level of cultivation is only 1,7% allocation, which places the region at its last position as regards all regional operational programmes. [[Is there anything you can do about it?]] The overall low level of payments also refers to the Regional Operational Programme of Warmia-Mazurskie Voivodship, where payments made for PLN 58 million from EU funds represent 0,5% allocation for the programme. This is, of course, the correlation between contracting and payments. [[Is there anything you can do about it?]] There was also a question about how long the beneficiaries in Warmia-Mazurskie voivodship have to wait for the settlement of recruitments against the background of other regions. The Regional Operational Programme of Warmia-Mazurskie Voivodeship has so far announced 85 calls for project funding in the contest mode within all priority axes. The duration of the call, calculated from the first day to the last day of the application for funding, is actually different depending on the axis, action or sub-action - from 11 calendar days to up to 107 days. The average duration of recruitment under the RPO of the Warmia-Mazurskie Voivodeship is about 31 calendar days, while the average duration of recruitment under all 16 RPOs is about 60 calendar days. On average, the beneficiaries of the RPO of Warmińsko-Mazurskie Voivodeship are waiting 115 days to settle the recruitments, and on average in all voivodships this process lasts about 187 days. That's what it looks like right now. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u14	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I wanted to say this: at the moment, the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management is carrying out a verification of all these programmes. I would like to remind you that there was a "Kawka" programme, i.e. the elimination of low emissions supporting growth energy efficiency and the development of distributed renewable energy sources, as well as the "Fish" programme, i.e. the thermomodernisation of buildings. There was also the Prosumer programme, i.e. the use mainly of solar energy for heating purposes. The situation is that, unfortunately, the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management does not have enough resources to grant all these programmes. Action has therefore been taken to achieve a much better environmental and economic impact than has been the case so far. The problem so far was that the previous national management board of the Fund did not have a sound financial plan, which made the funds allocated to these programmes insufficient. The National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management in the first half of 2016 carried out a comprehensive review, evaluation and verification of its priority programmes, including in the field of atmospheric protection. The results of these in-depth analyses were used to prepare a new offer, taking into account all available national and foreign resources. In view of the overlap between the areas of support for the programmes: "Kawka', "Fish' and 'Prosumer', and the need to adapt the national offer of the fund to the real needs and conditions of the regions, it was decided to create a new, uniform and flexible support mechanism for the projects so far financed under the three above. programs. As agreed at the Convention of the Governor of the Provincial Funds for Environmental Protection and Water Management, which took place at the end of June this year, the national fund decided to redirect these tasks to the voivodship funds that are closer to the local community. A new, comprehensive instrument called the Region to support the fight against low emissions will be addressed to the regions through individual voivodship funds. The terms and conditions of the new programme should be known in the fourth quarter of this year. Nevertheless, I would still like to say here that the national fund did not agree with the provision of financial resources in the third call for the programme of PLN 120 million and the recruitment for the programme of Ryss: the refusal for six voivodeship funds, the conclusions of the remaining four funds were not included in the budget of the call, which was PLN 100 million. Nevertheless, within the framework of the programme, the National Fund signed 22 agreements with 12 voivodship funds, for a total amount of PLN 174 million. Only two of these agreements have been fully financed and completed ecologically. The remaining 12 contracts are in progress. So far, only 74 million PLN has been paid out, and 100 million PLN remains to be paid. I just wanted to stress that these programmes were inefficient if they were not implemented despite the grants. In the first two editions almost 640 million PLN was released, 60 cities benefited, 35 thousand were liquidated. coal kilns and 9,000. low emission heat sources. It is much better to implement some comprehensive solutions such as the expansion of district heating networks than to replace individual furnaces, which unfortunately gives little environmental effect. Nevertheless, the voivodship funds may continue to do such things, but within their resources, as well as in preferential loans. I would also like to say that the Minister of Energy Mr Krzysztof Tchórzewski has signed an agreement with the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management on comprehensive advice on reducing, among others. low emissions. More detailed information will be provided in writing because my time is running out right now. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This is in line with the Budget Act. Therefore, the draft law proposes a one-year increase in the level of the special subsidy allocated to the activities of the SDS. On the other hand, the Social Assistance Act states that the voivodeship annually fixes the average monthly amount of the grant per participant of the support centre for people with mental disorders, including for ŚDS at a rate of not less than 250% , and for self-help clubs of not less than 80%. As a result of the proposed amendment to the Around Budget Act, the level of the special subsidy in 2017 for the activities of support centres for people with mental disorders will be at the level of 2016, i.e. 200%. , with an increase in 2016 compared to 2015, will be 230% in 2018 and 250% in 2019 In accordance with the amendment of the Social Assistance Act. It should therefore be made clear that this proposed change in no way constitutes a reduction in the funding of SDSs, will only result in a one-year shift in the level of grants. I would like to stress that the execution of alimony is carried out by bailiffs on the basis of the Civil Procedure Code and the Bill on bailiffs and the implementing rules for this bill. It is the effectiveness of judicial bailiffs that depends primarily on the level of the execution of alimony. The Minister for Family, Labour and Social Policy does not have the means to influence the effectiveness and manner in which bailiffs execute judicial decisions, including alimony decisions. The issue of the execution of alimony by bailiffs, as well as the provisions of the Penal Code that penalize the phenomenon of non-alination, is a matter for the Minister of Justice. However, under the jurisdiction of the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy under the Law of 7 September 2007 on assistance to persons entitled to maintenance, material support shall be provided to persons entitled to maintenance who do not receive them because of the ineffectiveness of the execution. This support is then granted from the maintenance fund for persons meeting the statutory criteria. The income criterion which entitles you to benefits from the maintenance fund, as it was said, is PLN 725 net. Height ww. the income criterion for entitlement to maintenance fund benefits may be amended under the Law on assistance to persons entitled to maintenance. On the other hand, I would like to stress very strongly that the benefits from the maintenance fund, because here is the essence, are in principle feedback. The State, through the maintenance fund, lends to the maintenance debtor, guaranteeing that the maintenance creditor receives the statutory maintenance. Thus, in practice benefits from the maintenance fund are benefits financed by the State budget, since the rate of reimbursement of these benefits from maintenance debtors is low in effectiveness. She was 13% in 2015. In 2015, almost PLN 1.5 billion was spent on maintenance benefits from the state budget, while debtors paid back around 193 million. Therefore, given that the maintenance fund benefits are paid from the State budget, it must be concluded that it is currently not possible to refer this form to all persons entitled to maintenance who do not receive them because of the - it must be stressed - the inefficiencies of execution. Therefore, the availability of these benefits is conditioned, among others. the fulfilment of the income criterion. It should also be stressed that this increase, possibly an increase in the income criterion, is therefore linked to the rate of return - what I said was that this return was the essence - by maintenance debtors already paid benefits from the maintenance fund, and this in turn depends mainly on the effectiveness of executions carried out by bailiffs. In view of the above, it must be said that work is not being done in this direction at this stage. However, it must be said that we are in contact with the Ministry of Justice in order to develop comprehensive solutions to counter the phenomenon of non-alimentation. The policy direction is not to increase the income criterion and to finance it from the state budget, on the contrary, to change public awareness. This is why we want to have a social campaign this year to raise the issue of non-alimentation and encourage the payment of alimony. At the same time, it should be pointed out that on 16 September of the same year the Ministry of Justice referred to the agreement a draft amendment to the Penal Code law, which amends the Penalization Penalization of the Article. 209 k.k.a. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u40	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I ask the question: Is the Ministry planning to take action to create legislation aimed at harmonising supervisory decisions issued by voivodships across the country? The rules of common law are often opaque, give rise to differences of interpretation and are interpreted differently, which consequently leads to the issuing of completely different supervisory decisions by different voivodships in the country. Very often the provisions of a resolution adopted by a given city council are practically a copy of a solution functioning in another city, located in another voivodship. Attempting to adopt a similar resolution often ends with a declaration of nullity, at the same time binding in another city, in another voivodship of the resolution of the same content - where the voivode did not see any shortcomings in the decision taken or felt that the resolution contained legal decisions. Many times, with the same resolutions taken in two different cities of the same voivodship, in one case the voivodship, in a common way, passes the resolution, and in another resolution is challenged by the voivod in the procedure of supervisory decision. This creates uncertainty about the rules created at local government level, as well as leads to a legislative mess, because in various voivodships there are different interpretations of the rules and different content of supervisory decisions issued. One way to solve this problem could be to adopt a regulation that would create a central register of supervisory decisions issued by voivodships, which would inform about the supervisory decisions issued. After all, the legislator introduced mechanisms for harmonising tax interpretations. In addition, it would be appropriate to create a regulation which would prohibit the issuing of different decisions on matters already dealt with in supervisory decisions. This solution would guarantee the uniformity and regularity of the voivodship's decisions, and would enable the municipalities to carry out their tasks effectively. These are examples of proposals. Of course, if there are any other proposals that will solve the problem, they will certainly also be with huge [[♪ The bell ♪]] welcomed by local governments in our country. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u41	Thank you very much. And I'm asking the Minister to answer.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u42	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, In reply, it can be said at the outset that the actual problem of the diversity of judgments exists, but this is a deeper problem. I'm in a hurry to explain that according to the article. 98 of the Municipal Government Act, the decision of the supervisory authority concerning the municipality is subject to appeal to the administrative court, because of the unlawfulness, of course, within 30 days of the date of service. According to the article, of course. 170 The law on proceedings before administrative courts is binding not only on the parties and on the court which issued them, but also on other courts, other public authorities and, in the cases provided for in the law, on other persons. The decisions of the supervisory authority are therefore subject to verification by the administrative court to which their final assessment falls. In view of the fact that administrative courts in different voivodships have to issue different judgments in similar cases, the supervisory authorities are required to respect final decisions and to take into account often uneven judicial lines. They therefore take different decisions. There may be a situation in which the supervisory decision taken by the voivodship in the same factual and legal state in one voivodship will not be challenged by the administrative courts, and in another voivodship the administrative court will repeal it. It should be noted, however, that each of the decisions taken falls for a specific factual situation and, of course, refers to a given act of a local government unit. It is also worth noting that the resolution or order of the municipal authority may be challenged not only by the supervisory decision of the voivode but also directly by the judgment of the administrative court, by a complaint brought by the voivode or by a citizens' complaint, in accordance with Article. Article 101 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Municipal Government Act. In this context, the introduction of a regulation prohibiting the issuing of different supervisory decisions by voivodships could be incompatible with the underlying Article. 184 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland judicial control of the activities of public administration. On the other hand, with regard to the creation of a central register of supervisory decisions issued by voivodships, it can be noted that at present there are tools that support voivodship services in the implementation of these supervisory powers. The staff of the voivodship offices are equipped with special legal software enabling them to see the current state of the case law. Moreover, problems related to interpretation and, as a result, their application, which arise when verifying the legality of acts of authorities of local government units, are discussed at regular meetings of representatives of the legal supervision cells of the voivodship offices, which are often attended by judges of administrative courts. Information on supervisory decisions taken shall also be available in the voivodship official journals where, in accordance with the provisions of Article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 8a The Act on the Publication of Normative Acts and Certain Other Acts is hereby published, inter alia. supervisory decisions relating to acts of local law of authorities representing local self-government units. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Dear Members, In the MMA's assessment, it would be useful to examine the issue of software creation which could be helpful, used by voivods in the procedure for verifying the legality of acts of authorities of local self-government and to have a positive impact on maintaining the uniformity of supervisory decisions as far as possible in the same cases, and the importance of considering this proposal for the development of such a system will, as always, have financial considerations, but we will examine this problem. Thank you very much.

I would like to start by saying that there are two key principles for implementing complex IT projects, those relating to public administration. These are two principles, the failure to comply with which caused inefficient spending of funds on computerisation of the administration, which we had at our disposal in the years 2007 and 2013 from the Operational Programme. These two key golden rules are like this. One: IT projects must be implemented if we have a stable legislative environment, i.e. are clear, transparent legislation, all possible implementing regulations are issued and the computerisation of administrative processes is implemented in relation to a stable legislative environment. The second key condition for the success of IT projects carried out by the government is that, if this is a project whose interests are several or several public administrations, it is necessary to coordinate the conduct of these public administrations and to cooperate between key stakeholders in this project. My answer will start with the fact that as far as the CEPiK 2.0 project is concerned, which was initiated in September 2013, neither one or the other of these golden rules for the implementation of the IT project was respected in its design and implementation. Let us remember that this is a project whose implementation will be one of the most extensive IT systems in Poland affecting citizens and the functioning of administration, because its extensive and complexness will make it affect many stakeholders. These activities were intended to assess the feasibility of the project as being able to integrate all those stakeholders involved in the project. The actions that have been implemented since January 2016 are the resumption of communication between key stakeholders of the project, which died in October 2015. License plate and 5,000. driver's licenses a day. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u71	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! Self-governments have been concerned about improving educational infrastructure for years. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u72	You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u73	Mr. Marshal! Membership! Thank you very much, Madam President, for this question, because it is important. Hopefully, the information we've been passing on again... Because in various forms, the Ministry of Education answers the burning questions of local government officials and more. I think that today's answer will also reach the actors and calm the mood a bit. The change in the structure of schools announced by the Ministry of National Education, including the phasing out of secondary education, does not jeopardise the sustainability of projects funded by European funds to support education. The sustainability requirements are governed by Regulation No 1303/2013 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 17 December 2013 laying down common provisions on the European Regional Development Fund, the European Social Fund, the Cohesion Fund, the European Agricultural Fund for Rural Development and the European Maritime and Fisheries Fund and laying down general provisions on the European Regional Development Fund, the European Social Fund, the Cohesion Fund and the European Maritime and Fisheries Fund and repealing Council Regulation No 1083/2006 - that is the legal basis. According to Art. 71 Ww. a regulation in the case of an operation involving investment in a production structure or investment shall be reimbursed from the European Fund for Strategic Investments if, during a period of 5 years from the final payment to the beneficiary or during a period determined in accordance with the State aid rules, there are any of the following circumstances: firstly, the cessation of the production activity or the transfer thereof outside the programme area; second case, the change in ownership of the structure element which confers undue advantages on the undertaking or public entity; and third case, a material change affecting the nature of the operation, its objectives or the conditions of implementation which could lead to a breach of its original objectives. It should be stressed at this point that the main objective of the intervention in the field of education is to equip pupils with skills to function effectively on the labour market. This objective is implemented at every level of education in schools and educational establishments - from the European Social Fund. School equipment is only a means of achieving this objective and is of a subsidiary nature to activities aimed at pupils. Changes in the structure of the education system should therefore not jeopardise the sustainability of interventions made through support from European funds, including investment in secondary education infrastructure. A smooth and efficient introduction of the new structure of education requires a transitional period of several years. This will enable the current education system to adapt to new systemic solutions. The decision on the form and time of the transformation of the junior high school belongs to the competence of the local government conducting the secondary school, the legal person not being a local government unit or the natural person running the existing public or non-public secondary school. The changes will start with the 2017/2018 school year. At that time, pupils leaving the school year 2016/2017 VI primary school will become pupils in the seventh grade of primary school. I'm gonna start with this. the gradual elimination of junior high, there will be no recruitment to junior high. In the school year 2018/2019 the last year of class III children will graduate from middle school. As of 1 September 2019, middle school will not function in the school system. As a result, the obligations arising from the need to maintain the sustainability of projects supporting secondary education can be transferred to other schools, educational establishments, bodies leading within the framework of legal succession, provided that the project's objectives continue to be pursued. And that's the essence of the case and the questions you're asking today. This solution will avoid the need for reimbursement of grants granted to schools. The commitments made by the beneficiaries in this respect will require a detailed analysis of how they can continue to be used to achieve their educational objectives. In addition, the marshal offices, all of them, should analyse the potential risks to the intervention envisaged for implementation in the area of education in the coming months and inform applicants of the need to take into account the impact of the reform in the planned actions. They should also use a model grant agreement containing clauses on the possibility of amending the project in the event of external causes, inter alia. independent of the beneficiary, e.g. changes in existing law. In order to maintain the principle of sustainability and effective investment of European Union funds, these guidelines [[♪ The bell ♪]] have been transferred to all marshals throughout the country, the managing bodies of regional operational programmes. At the same time, they are still the subject of analyses by the Ministry of Development and the Ministry of National Education. Regional authorities will be kept informed of new developments. Thank you. [[Applause]]

I would like to point out that the tax system is the main source of revenue of local government units, the revenue of the state budget and is treated as such with due seriousness by the Government of the Republic of Poland. However, it must be acknowledged, and we all know that economic reality, number, size of economic and financial transactions is growing all the time. It is a fact and a constant feature that it is very diverse. It changes over time, the needs of the market change, investors' ambitions change, but also laws change, because tax law according to the Constitution, with Article. 217, in fact, creates the Sejm, but a large part of this activity of the Sejm is the result of the rules that are created in the European Parliament. Within the framework of harmonisation, Poland is also required to adapt Polish legislation to what direction They change. It must also be admitted objectively that in all countries this problem of simplification of legislation seems to exist and probably everywhere, and every time it is an ideal that should be pursued, but it is difficult to say that someone has succeeded in simplifying this tax system that has led to taxpayers to a satisfactory degree. However, of course, all the time we try to do everything and fulfil these expectations of taxpayers to the fullest extent, although it must also be said that we are also surrounded by judgments of courts, which often in similar cases geographically, which is included in the question, are based on various arguments and make different judgments. Then the interpretations we make must consume all of this and often keep up with it in order to meet this ambition of clarity of the laws, their obviousness and consistency. In these circumstances, we try to function and provide uniform, consistent, clear, clear rules for those who need them to conduct business. I would like to draw your attention to the fact that yesterday there was a reading of the National Tax Administration Act, in short called the KAS. We hope that it will be received kindly in the Seym and worked quite quickly. It contains an important change, i.e. that an entity such as National Tax Information will be created, different from that which today operates in this sphere, i.e. National Tax Information, which will have a centralised structure. As today, the five centres that operate in this area have the competence to inform taxpayers throughout the tax area and answer their questions, so after the reform, five centres will be centralised and specialised, but there will be uniform answers across the country. This is a great progress and a great hope connected with the reform, the framework of which is defined by this law on the National Tax Administration, which I was talking about. It seems to be a step in the right direction. On the other hand, today I would also like to point out that it is possible for taxpayers and employees of the tax administration to have a correct and uniform interpretation of the tax legislation to be familiar with the interpretation in the Official Journal of the Minister of Finance in the Public Information Bulletin. In addition, all interpretations of tax legislation, including general interpretations, are included in the tax information system, so-called. the SIP database, which is available to the staff of the bodies subordinate to the Finance Minister, and copies of each general interpretation issued shall be sent to all the directors of the tax chambers and the directors of the tax control offices. It seems, therefore, that such a base as that mentioned by the Member exists and is to be used. Sometimes you just have to reach out to her. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u142	Thank you, Member. I'm asking the Minister to answer.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u143	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! With regard to this well-known problem - which, unfortunately, has existed for several years and has recently become much worse - it must be said that the main cause is, unfortunately, the economic gains that have been consumed by the networks, and this way of circumvention has also extended to both non-public health establishments and hospital pharmacys, although we do not know the scale of the treatment in relation to hospital pharmacies. The Chief Pharmaceutical Inspector shall monitor the functioning of pharmaceutical wholesalers and the issue of drug exports. In the event of the occurrence of the so-called. the reversed distribution chain - both in relation to clinics and private health establishments - the voivodships of pharmaceutical inspectors and the chief pharmaceutical inspector shall transmit, in accordance with Article. 87. 6 of the Pharmaceutical Law Act, information to authorities keeping records of medicinal entities, and in particular to voivodships. Such information shall also be communicated to the health minister, tax or fiscal control authorities, depending on the supervisory and control powers and the nature of those irregularities. If information has been obtained on the existence of such a phenomenon, the Chief Pharmaceutical Inspector shall also have the possibility to refer to law enforcement authorities a notice of suspicion of an offence under Article. 126b of the Pharmaceutical Law Act. It should be noted that, under the Pharmaceutical Law, the marketing of medicinal products can only be carried out on the principles clearly described in that Act. The marketing rights of medicinal products shall be those of the manufacturers responsible for the distribution of their own products, pharmaceutical wholesalers as well as pharmacies, pharmacies and non-pharmacies. In view of the provisions of the Pharmaceutical Law itself and of the 2002 Regulation of the Minister of Health on entities authorised to purchase medicinal products in a pharmaceutical wholesaler, we can conclude that a medicinal product may be supplied by a medicinal product in wholesalers and pharmacies in general, provided that these products are necessary in view of the nature of the health services provided - that is to say, they are purchased only for use in accordance with Article. 68 of the Pharmaceutical Law Act, not to carry out any other operations, including secondary trading outside the medicinal facility. The second case, which is a specific exception to the prohibition on the transfer of these medicines in the course of a therapeutic activity, is Article. 106 Act, which states that hospital pharmacists may supply medicinal products to medical professionals, such as fixed and 24-hour health benefits or such: outpatient health benefits under a contract concluded with those entities, provided that this does not adversely affect the operation of the pharmacy itself. The second option is to supply patients included in treatment programmes under the 2011 Refund Act, as well as patients receiving outpatient chemotherapy. And only these two forms are not treated as pharmaceutical products within the meaning of the Pharmaceutical Law. A check which aims to verify medicines given to patients in the framework of health services provided, a registered body for such an establishment may carry out on its own on the basis of the Act on medicinal activities. In the context of checks, persons authorised by the authority keeping the register of medicinal operators shall have the right, inter alia, to do so. access to all premises of a medical operator or access to medical records. This means that the controlled registry authority itself has tools that enable it to determine whether the physically contained medicinal products are present in the premises of the controlled entity in such a quantity and under the conditions under which they should be stored, i.e. the authority has the possibility to verify that the amount of medicinal products in the hospital pharmacy is in line with the marketing process. In addition, it is possible to determine whether the medical records of the recipients reflect both the name and the amount of these measures, i.e. on this basis it can be concluded that the healthcare providers may have mis-trafficked the medicines. According to the pharmacy authority with which the Ministry of Health is in close contact, any illegal export case should have a final in the disciplinary court, which should punish dishonest pharmacists. On the other hand, the effectiveness of such penalties is low. We are currently introducing a monitoring system for the marketing of medicinal products, it is in phase [[♪ The bell ♪]] A test. We hope that such a mechanism will help to increase surveillance. A revision of the Pharmaceutical Law, which increases the powers of pharmacists, may also give an opportunity to improve supervision of illicit drug trafficking. This amendment is also underway at the Ministry of Health. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u154	Thank you, Member. I ask the Minister to respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u155	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In answer to your questions, whether other plans relating to the necessary changes are being prepared outside the Code of Good Sponsor Practices, I kindly inform you that the Ministry of Treasury, with the support of the Ministry of Sport and Tourism, has developed a document This document proposes solutions for State Treasury companies planning sponsorship activities. These activities should be undertaken by companies in a transparent and responsible manner, following the fulfilment of the long-term economic and financial stability condition and assuming measurable results. This document takes the form of voluntary regulation which provides the basis for implementing the practices recorded there. Good practices propose using the potential to create a positive image by participating in projects carried out under the auspices of government administrations, and in particular by participating in projects of a national or transnational nature. For the first time, detailed recommendations were presented on the provisions of sponsorship agreements, including in particular the clauses enabling the sponsor to monitor, i.e. to supervise the expenditure of the sponsor and the provisions guaranteeing the appropriate allocation of funds. According to the document announced, funding should be used exclusively for training rather than for the maintenance of frequent administration and bureaucracy. An intra-corporate legal framework for sponsorship has also been defined and sponsorship areas have been defined, in particular: physical education, professional sport, sport of children and young people, cultural activities, science and public education, environmental initiatives and social actions. The Ministry of Sport and Tourism was involved in the implementation of this project by preparing for Polish sports unions a model questionnaire annexed to the application for sport sponsorship. By 18 July this year. 26 Polish Olympic sports associations submitted applications. In accordance with the practice adopted by treasure and sport resorts, the documentation in question has been evaluated by the internal team which prepared the relevant opinions. The Minister of Sport and Tourism gave appropriate recommendations for 11 Polish sports unions, while informing the public about this fact by publishing these requests on the Ministry's website. Currently, meetings with Polish sports unions are held in the sports department to sum up and evaluate the implementation of the preparation programme and launch of our representatives in Rio de Janeiro. The scope and level of funding of the activities of Polish sports unions in view of the start of the Olympic Games in Tokyo in 2020 will depend to a certain extent on the medalability of the unions. We intend to financially support the most powerful ones, create opportunities for forward-looking relationships and oblige the weakest ones to prepare development strategies with specific objectives for all stages of their implementation and the resulting tasks. However, it should be noted that in connection with the starting election campaign in Polish sports unions development strategies and directions of actions of individual unions may be modified. As regards the question of what legislative changes must take place in order to start implementing this necessary plan for the introduction of the Code of Good Practice, I would like to inform you that this document takes the form of voluntary regulation. Therefore, statutory amendments to the launch of a plan relating to the introduction of good sponsoring practices appear to be pointless. Turning to the next question, which criteria will guide the Ministry of Sport and Tourism in verifying those sports that should be prioritised and supported under the Code of Good Practice, I would just like to complement my earlier statement. The criteria contained in the model questionnaire annexed to the application for sport sponsorship are elements of the content assessment of the opinion of the ensemble and the basis for the recommendation given by the Ministry of Sport and Tourism. These are among others.: a detailed description of the proposed task, including the date and place of its implementation, the identification of the main and stage objectives of the task, including training and organisation, the presentation of the schedule of actions, the cost of tasks and the expected financial contribution of the sponsor, the description of past achievements in the international arena and sports assumptions for the next sports season and multiannual training, the applicant's organisational experience, the identification of the media value and mutual benefit of cooperation with the sponsor, information on the applicant's financial situation, risk analysis and development strategies of the sport concerned, studies on the mediaity of sport and sponsorship offers. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u315	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report by the Joint Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Foreign Affairs on the Government's draft law on ratification of the Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of the Republic of Belarus on cooperation in the field of disaster prevention, natural disasters, other serious accidents and the removal of their consequences, signed in Bialystok on 23 April 2015. The Republic of Belarus is the only neighbouring country with the People's Republic, with which we do not have a government agreement governing matters relating to the provision and acceptance of mutual assistance in emergency situations. Currently, the Republic is party to 10 similar agreements. These are agreements with neighbouring countries, but also agreements with other countries in the region, e.g. this is a contract with Slovenia or a contract with Hungary. The agreement with the Republic of Belarus has been negotiated for a very long time, because since 2001. And the negotiations ended in 2013. Finally, the agreement was signed on 23 April 2015 in Bialystok by the Minister for Emergency Situations of the Republic of Belarus, Mr Vladimir Waszczenko, and by the Secretary of State in the Ministry of Interior of Mr Piotr Stachańczyk. I am pleased that the current government has maintained its decision to ratify this agreement. High Izbo! Currently, the official rescue services of each country can operate only on their territory, i.e. in practice to the border. The conclusion of the agreement will positively increase the level of security in both countries, in particular in border areas, by reducing the time of entry to rescue operations in the event of a disaster, natural disaster or other serious accident. The agreement will be applied in crisis situations to save life, health, property and the environment, but cooperation will also cover the exchange of information on all situations that pose a risk of dangerous events, e.g. industrial accidents in facilities located in the border area. Unfortunately, such tragic incidents in the border area happen every now and then. I think it is worth recalling in the High House the accident that took place in 2009. Then the Border Guard helicopter, which took off from Białystok, crashed and fell on the Belarusian side, 200 meters from our border. Three people died at the time. The actions of the fire department on the Belarusian side were only possible after the necessary arrangements had been made. I hope that situations which will make it necessary to use the agreement will be extremely rare, but it is very important that we do everything possible in this House to ensure that the formalities for rescue operations are as low as possible. As rapporteur, I will also mention that during the work of the committee there was a discussion with Minister Zieliński. All participants in this discussion advocated the need for cooperation between emergency services of neighbouring countries and mutual assistance. High Izbo! The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Foreign Affairs unanimously voted for ratification of the Agreement. [[Applause]]

Natural, industrial or environmental disasters do not know borders, they spread very quickly. Only joint action by neighbouring countries can effectively stop them and, importantly, minimise their effects. Border areas with Belarus are areas of large forest complexes, of course with a fire risk. A fire in such a vast area is very difficult to control and, of course, it threatens an ecological disaster. In such situations, it is necessary to combine the forces and resources of the rescue services of both countries in order to take action quickly and effectively. In practice, after the entry into force of these provisions, Polish firefighters will be able to exceed on a simplified basis the Belarusian border to take part in action on the other side, and vice versa. Currently, the official services of each country can act exclusively on their territory, i.e. to the border. The Agreement sets out the principles for voluntary assistance in the event of disasters, natural disasters and other serious accidents and the scope of cooperation in developing and taking preventive measures and removing their consequences. As I said at the beginning, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club supports this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

What is more, sometimes the fastest rescue can come not from the country in which the event occurred, but from the neighbouring country, for example through. better conditions of access or such phenomena. No one, therefore, needs to be convinced of the importance of the proper cooperation of emergency services to minimise or prevent the effects of the above mentioned events. Consequently, it is necessary and necessary to conclude a procedated agreement which lays down rules for the granting and acceptance of mutual assistance and regulates cooperation in the field of rescue and civil protection, especially as in crisis situations a very important role is played by the passage of time, which properly defined and coordinated cooperation between the various services allows to minimise, thus increasing the possibility of preventing the effects of various disasters, possibly the extent of these effects. There is no doubt, therefore, that the Republic of Poland should have such a cooperation agreement in emergency situations with all its neighbours, including the Republic of Belarus, in which case we did not have this agreement. So this omission needs to be made up as soon as possible. It should also be ratified so that cooperation between the relevant services of the two countries can be initiated as soon as possible and that they can be optimised and maximised in the event of environmental disasters, natural disasters, accidents and other situations which pose a risk to the lives, health and property of citizens covered by this agreement. The agreement in question contains all the necessary annexes and sufficiently defines the scope of mutual cooperation, including the definition of the rules for the granting of aid. [[♪ The bell ♪]] in the event of disasters and natural disasters. It shall specify the authorities responsible for those tasks and the rules and documents empowering rescue services to cross the state border, ensuring a rapid response, the movement of persons and the carriage of funds by both land, air and water. In addition, it sets out clearly the principles and limits of this cooperation. This guarantees the proper security of both parties, as opposed to e.g. A terrible bill, the famous 1066 act about the so-called. Helping my brother. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u348	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear three minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Dorota Arciszewska-Mielewczyk, Law and Justice have the floor. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u349	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I am pleased to present my position on the Government's draft law on ratification of the 2014 amendments to the Maritime Labour Convention. The amendments to the Maritime Labour Convention 2006 were adopted on 11 April 2014 by the International Labour Organisation's Special Tripartite Committee and subsequently approved by the International Labour Conference on 11 June 2014. They were notified jointly by representatives of seafarers and shipowners. Representatives were involved in the work of the Special Tripartite Committee, among others. International Shipping Chamber, clubs, maritime insurers with more than 90% insurance World fleet, BIMCO - Baltic and International Maritime Council, i.e. Baltic and International Maritime Council, and ITF trade unions. Polish trade unions and the Polish Shipowners' Union were also in favour of the amendments. They therefore have the support of all sides of the social dialogue. Amendments amend Regulation 2.5: Reparation and Regulation 4.2: The liability of shipowners with regard to MLC, and its annexes, make it compulsory for shipowners to have insurance or other financial security for seafarers' claims in the event of their abandonment by the shipowners and in the event of death or long-term disability resulting from an accident at work or occupational disease. In addition, the 2014 amendments introduce an obligation for seafarers to carry on board documents showing that the ship owner has such financial security and specify the requirements for those documents. In accordance with the procedure laid down in MLC, the amendments will enter into force on 18 January 2017. The Maritime Labour Convention of 2006 entered into force on 20 August 2013. The Parties to the Convention are 74 States representing a total of 91% world tonnage. The Convention was implemented into Polish law only in the Law of 5 August 2015 on Maritime Labour. This law entered into force in November 2015, which means that Polish seafarers were deprived of the protection provided for in the Convention for more than 2 years. Moreover, the 2015 Maritime Labour Act was prepared in violation of the principles of social dialogue, without listening to the demands of the parties to this dialogue, both employers and workers. As a result, the Maritime Labour Act adopted in 2015 needs to be amended. For this reason, a working group was established at the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping for the revision of the Maritime Labour Act. It is also important to emphasise the adoption by the Government of Law and Justice of the Act of 24 August 2006 on tonnage tax. For 8 years of government, the Civic Platform has not taken steps to create the conditions for registering ships in Poland. For this reason, only a few vessels will be affected by the subjective amendments, which are currently raising the white and red flag. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Therefore, one of the first steps of the PiS government was the adoption of the Act of 6 July 2016 on the activation of the shipbuilding industry and complementary industries prepared by the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u351	Thank you kindly. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Minister! The Maritime Labour Convention is undeniably the first maritime labour code, which includes more than 1 million seafarers working worldwide, a large number of shipowners, as well as other entities that are related to the maritime industry around the world. The maritime transport sector is the first to require action by international organisations to regulate existing international legal standards. These regulations have been introduced and have affected the functioning of the so-called wide-ranging industry. Shipping, and they've created a global maritime code. The implementation of the provisions of the Convention ensured not only the opportunity for all seafarers to have fair and harmonised employment conditions, but also a normative basis for working in line with safety standards. The provisions of the Convention shall apply to international maritime transport widely understood, regulate and cover essential issues relating to mutual rights and obligations between shipowners, intermediaries and seafarers. The regulations of the Maritime Labour Convention have an impact on workers' relations through the numerous guarantees contained therein. The Convention guarantees adequate living conditions for seafarers on board and provides wide-ranging medical and social protection measures or forms of legal protection. It should be noted that Poland ratified the Maritime Labour Convention on 3 May 2012 and that the Convention entered into force on 20 August 2013. It is worth recalling that more than 70 States are parties to the Convention, which represent more than 90% in total. the global tonnage of the fleet. It should also be noted that the first amendment contained in this proposal relates to the repatriation of seafarers and provides that a State party to the Convention will provide ships flying its flag with a rapid and effective financial security system to assist seafarers in the event of their abandonment. The security scheme indicated in the Convention may take the form of a social security or insurance scheme or a national fund or be established in another similar form. The second amendment concerning the so-called: Regulation 4.2 lays down minimum requirements for national and financial security legislation to cover the payment of any contractual claims which it covers and which arise during its validity. In view of the validity of these amendments, which guarantee the financial security and support of seafarers during work when repatriating, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club is in favour of this bill. At the same time, we expect the government to implement the necessary provisions [[♪ The bell ♪]] without undue delay. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u354	Thank you very much. Mr Piotr Misilo, the Modern Posel Club, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u355	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! This Convention is a legal act bringing together the comprehensive standards contained in almost all of the more than 60 existing conventions and recommendations of the International Labour Organisation for Maritime Labour adopted by 2006. It is therefore referred to as consolidated. In view of the comprehensive nature of the regulation, the Convention has been recognised as the fourth pillar of the international regime governing maritime transport. The Convention sets out the requirements for seafarers to be able to work on board ships, the rules for the provision of placements, working and living conditions on board ships, health and social protection requirements, and includes provisions to ensure effective enforcement of the Convention. The Convention has so far ratified 79 countries representing 91% world tonnage. The amendments to the Convention introduced on 11 June 2014 concern two issues: ensuring financial security for abandoned seafarers and ensuring financial security for seafarers' claims relating to accident compensation, death, long-term disability as a result of damage or sickness to work on board the ship. A seafarer shall be deemed to have been abandoned if the ship owner fails to cover the costs of repatriation, leaves the seafarer without means of subsistence, unilaterally fails to fulfil his duties towards the seafarer, and in particular does not pay him a salary for at least 2 months. The amendments made to the Convention provide that Member States shall require shipowners to have a financial security to cover the abovementioned. claims in the form of private insurance or insurance under the social security scheme or a national fund or other equivalent security. In addition, the ship owner will have to document the possession of the above mentioned. security with a certificate in accordance with the model introduced by the amendment to the Convention. As a consequence, the ship owner is to obtain a financial security in the event that the ship owner fails to comply with the relevant obligation at the request of the seafarer, he shall immediately obtain the benefit available to him. At this point, it is worth noting that the financial security for late wages is limited to the amount available for 4 months. Shipowners do not currently have insurance that would cover the above obligations. Second line. The financial security is to be in the same form as in the case of a guarantee in the event of abandonment of a seafarer. Also in this case, it is envisaged that the vessel owner must have on board a document proving that he has obtained a security issued in accordance with a specified standard. The security system is to meet the following requirements: the payment of claims in full and without delay, in the event of difficulties in determining the amount of full compensation is to take place [[♪ The bell ♪]] partial payment - advance. A seafarer or a member of his immediate family or another person appointed by a seafarer may make a claim. The receipt of the benefit by the seafarer shall be confirmed by a statement the model of which forms an annex to the Convention. Most shipowners have adequate insurance in the scope of this obligation. As Poland did not object to the amendments to the Convention until 18 July 2016, they will enter into force in respect of Poland on 18 January 2017. Modern will vote in favour of this law. Madam President, of course this law has nothing to do with the activation of the shipbuilding industry, but it is obvious. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The issue of CIT, i.e. corporation tax on shipowners. Polish legislation allows shipowners to apply a flat-rate tax on tonnage in exchange for income tax. This solution is also beneficial. The draft law on women's rights and informed parenthood presented by me has been based on the demands of non-governmental organisations working for women's rights for many years. Reproduction rights, understood as the right to protect reproductive health and the right of self-determination in matters of procreation, belong to the canon of fundamental human rights. They are also protected by the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, ordering respect for the right to dignity, art. 30, guaranteeing everyone the right to legal protection of private, family life and to decide on their personal life, art. 47, right to health care, Article. 68, and the protection of motherhood and parenthood, Art. 18. However, these constitutional guarantees in the field of reproductive rights are usually under-insufficient by ordinary legislation. The regulation we are designing is designed to change this state of affairs. Firstly, we propose that reproductive rights are human rights, and informed parenting is not just the right to terminate pregnancy. It is above all the right to know about your sexuality, that is, sexual education, the right to decide when, how much and at what intervals you want to have children, or refunded contraception. Therefore, our project regulates matters related to the right to information, education, guidance and measures to make decisions on informed parenting - i.e. art. 1 - by: providing children and young people with access to knowledge about informed parenting, human health and sexuality; ensuring the availability of modern methods and measures to prevent pregnancy; creating the possibility of legal and safe abortion; providing a pregnant woman with full health care, e.g. through access to prenatal studies and classes at birth school, as well as material care; ensuring that the provider who has signed a contract with the National Health Fund will carry out the contracted benefits. Secondly, we want to guarantee the implementation of reproductive rights and not just write them down. Therefore, Art. 2 of the bill contains a glossary of concepts defining concepts such as reproductive rights, medical procedure, procreation support, pregnancy prevention measures, informed parenting, abortion, prenatal examinations, healthcare for pregnant women, healthcare provider, according to the terminology of international documents and standards of the World Health Organisation. Art. 3 guarantees the right to self-determination in the field of reproduction and contains an exemplary, open list of public administration activities aimed at achieving the objective of protecting informed parenthood. Art. 4 obliges governmental and local authorities to ensure the implementation of reproductive rights. This article also clarifies the principles of cooperation with non-governmental organisations active in the field of reproductive rights, in accordance with the Act of 24 April 2003 on public benefit activities and voluntary activities. Art. 5 sets out the responsibilities of government and local authorities in the health care and assistance of pregnant women. The detailed scope, form and method of granting the benefits referred to in paragraph. 1, define the Council of Ministers by regulation. Paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 2 grants pregnant schoolgirls the right to take the leave and assistance necessary to complete their education. Art. 6 introduces knowledge of human sexuality of not less than 1 lesson hour per week into school curricula. This subject will, of course, be adapted to the age, cognitive abilities and needs of pupils and pupils and will apply from the first grade of primary school. The programme content is intended to include information on human sexuality, reproductive rights, protection against sexual violence, as well as methods and measures to prevent pregnancy, ways of protecting against sexually transmitted diseases, including HIV and AIDS, and shaping non-violence, partnership relationships and gender equality in society. Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development Education and upbringing in agreement with the Minister responsible for Education and Education. health will establish, by means of a regulation, the scope of teaching content and introduce into the teacher education system the above mentioned. Subject matter. The fact that this subject will be taught by those who are properly prepared will guarantee that reliable information will be objective and free from indoctrination, so that students will gain knowledge that will enable them to protect themselves from dangerous sexual behaviour and unplanned pregnancy in the future and effectively protect their health. Art. 7 requires government and local authorities to ensure real access to pregnancy prevention methods and measures. At the same time, in the mouth. The refund of contraceptives under the provisions of the Act of 12 May 2011 on the refund of medicines, foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses and medical devices shall be guaranteed. A refund is also to be granted for post-comital contraception measures, as defined by the pregnancy prevention measures provided for in Article. 2 of this draft law. The right to free contraception will also be given to people in difficult material situations. Such a formulation of the conditions for the availability of pregnancy prevention measures will not only allow access to modern methods and measures of family planning for more women, regardless of their economic status, but will also save women raped so that they can protect themselves from an unwanted pregnancy resulting from traumatic experiences. The experience of other countries shows that full availability of post-comital contraception has significantly contributed to reducing the number of unplanned pregnancies and abortion procedures, as women can benefit from it in an emergency situation, i.e. in the case of unprotected sex or when other methods of protection against unwanted pregnancy have failed. Paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: Article 4 7 obliges county authorities to set up and finance institutions providing guidance on informed parenting and other reproductive rights. The network of such facilities at district level will improve access to reproductive health protection. Art. The 8th proposed proposal regulates abortion. In line with the intention of the projectors, the decision to terminate pregnancy should be a woman's only decision, since the embryo is not a legal matter. A woman has the right to discontinue pregnancy without any restrictions during the first trimester of pregnancy. Your doctor will discontinue your pregnancy as early as possible, according to current medical knowledge, including pharmacological methods. The procedure should be performed no later than 72 hours after the woman has expressed her will. More than 12 weeks of pregnancy, the procedure will be possible if there are three well-known conditions: pregnancy is a risk to the life or health of the pregnant woman, there is a high likelihood of severe and irreversible damage to the foetus or an incurable disease threatening its life, pregnancy is the result of a prohibited act, i.e. point 3. In all these cases, the final decision to continue or discontinue pregnancy shall be taken by the woman after receiving medical information. In the cases referred to in paragraph. The termination of pregnancy shall be permitted up to 24 weeks in the cases referred to in paragraph 2. If pregnancy threatens the health and life of a woman, termination of pregnancy is permitted without restriction, paragraph 3 - up to the 18th week of pregnancy. In proposing these provisions, the designers relied on the current law on family planning, protection of the human foetus and conditions for the admissibility of abortion, which includes this closed catalogue of conditions, as well as the latest laws governing the right to terminate pregnancy adopted in Spain and Portugal. Art. 9 is intended to ensure respect for the dignity of minors or women wholly incapacitated with regard to their reproductive rights. Although it is the statutory representative who must consent to the abortion procedure, both the minor and the woman completely incapacitated must agree to this medical procedure. If the legal representative does not give his consent, the decision shall be taken by the court of custody. It is worth noting that minors over the age of 13 must give their written consent and minors under the age of 13 have the right to express their own opinion. In the case of an incapacitated woman, the written consent of that person is also required, unless the expression does not permit mental health. According to Art. 10 termination of pregnancy is a health service within the meaning of the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare. The purpose of this article is to provide comprehensive benefits to pregnant women, including those related to abortion. The Act obliges the provider who has concluded an agreement with the National Health Fund to do so and requires it to make the list of names of all doctors cooperating with the provider publicly available, who rely on the so-called. Reconciliation clause - Art. 9 The Act of 5 December 1996 on the professions of doctors and dentists - refuses to provide health services related to abortion. This list is to be made available to a woman at her individual request. It is to be mandatory, in the light of Article. Article 10 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 point 3, notification to the National Health Fund and publication in the Public Information Bulletin. In addition, the National Health Fund terminates the contract with the provider if it does not provide, despite the possibility, comprehensive health services related to the health care of pregnant women, including those related to abortion, and fails to comply with the obligations set out in Article. Article 10 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3. The objective of this Article. 10 is to guarantee the real availability of health services, in particular those related to abortion, and should contribute to the reduction of the doctor-abuse conscience clause. Art. 11 is a guarantee of secrecy in all matters arising from the new law. The purpose of this provision is to respect privacy. The responsibility for the disclosure of secrecy lies with the provider and the victim of the disclosure of professional secrecy in matters governed by the new law has the right to demand an adequate sum for compensation or compensation for the damage suffered. It is worth noting that there have already been cases of breach of medical confidentiality, exposure to the harm of patients, therefore the sanctions introduced should be effective, proportionate and dissuasive, so that in the future there is no such cases, and that the woman, the person benefiting from reproductive health advice and benefits, is simply better protected. Provisions contained in Article. The current right to the changes introduced by the project is adapted by 12 and 15. The Social Assistance Act of 12 March 2004 is amended in which Article. 39 (1) (a) The special allowance may also be used to cover the costs of purchasing pregnancy prevention measures. Art. 13 amends the Act of 5 December 1996 on competitions for doctors and dentists. An important change is to improve access for minors to contraception, as at the request of a minor, the doctor is required to provide pregnancy prevention services without the consent of the statutory representative, guardian and court. In addition, a doctor who refers to a clause of conscience must report it in writing in each treatment facility or in each healthcare provider with which he cooperates and to the body which keeps a register of individual medical practices. Such a provision will put an end to the usual practice so far when a doctor refuses to provide services at a public institution on the grounds of a conscience clause, and without any problem does it in a private institution for money. An important solution is the amendment to the Penal Code of Article. 156 § 1 paragraph 1, introduced in Article. 14, consisting of decriminalisation of sterilization carried out with the consent of the patient or patient and the repeal of Article. 157a Criminal Code. Art. 15 amends the Act on the Ombudsperson of Children's Rights by repealing the Article. 2, which defines a child as a human being from conception to adulthood, which is incompatible with other laws, including the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. Art. 16 repeals the law on family planning, protection of the human foetus and conditions for abortion of 7 January 1993 and Article. 17 sets the date of entry into force of the Act within 14 days of the date of publication. Teenagers learn about it from the Internet or from their peers. As a result, unwanted pregnancies among minors are increasingly occurring, which is a phenomenon that needs to be tackled radically and effectively. The second element that should be stressed is the provision of more access to contraception. Poland is a country of the European Union where the use of modern methods of contraception is at the lowest level. There are two reasons. One is the lack of sexual education I mentioned. The second barrier is the economic barrier - because contraceptives are simply expensive, not all can afford them. Therefore, we support the claim for contraception refund, which will reduce the number of abortions. And then there's the art. Ten of this bill. Secondly, we provide the full opportunity to save the mother's life. The project provides that all medical activities that save the life of the mother, even those that can indirectly lead to the death of the child, are acceptable. And here no one will require heroism from mothers. It is the mother who guarantees the life of the child. Here, however, we can also refer to the general principle of criminal law, Art. 26 § 1 and 2 of the Penal Code, this is the construction of a state of higher necessity. Here, of course, the doctor doesn't commit any crime if he saves his mother's life. Thirdly, with regard to the issue of prenatal examinations, the project confirms that the child is a recipient of medical care and has the right of the patient, including the right of parents to information on his behalf. And these prenatal studies, which you have been polite to mention here, are now controversial mainly for one reason - because they are the basis for an unpunished abortion. I would like to assure you that the project does not affect the situation of the mothers who have miscarried. A single miscarriage is never a crime, resulting from the general principles of criminal law. As far as he goes, of course... Forgive me for returning to my earlier thought. Of course, saving your mother's life also involves the situation of ectopic pregnancy. In such a situation, the doctor not only has the right, but also the obligation to save the mother's life, as the mother is the guarantee of the child's life. In addition, I would like to tell you that the Ordo Iuris Institute has prepared a bill to ensure that the quality of care for the mother and child is improved, for those children who are affected by a life-threatening disease or children who are disabled. This project has already been presented, it has been handed over to the Ministry of Health, it has been handed over to the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy and we hope that the work on this project will proceed quickly. In a nutshell, this whole aid package provides for support for perinatal hospices, care benefits without having to give up the work of parents of disabled children, the development of home long-term paediatric care, health benefits, non-material support, psychological assistance and support benefits, the faster adoption of a child conceived as a result of a prohibited act or a disabled child, the development of interventional adoption centres and the reform of the functioning of single mothers' homes by abolishing their regionalisation. Penal Code and the Act of 25 February 1964. I ask Mr Jan Klawiter to present the justification for the project.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-22-sejm-26-2.u601	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The bill, which I have the honour of presenting today, has two simple objectives. The first objective is to guarantee children born in vitro, eliminate selection practices, restore the relationship between the decision to conceive life and the responsibility for its development. This responsibility is all the more important because, in the case of in vitro practice, there are no accidental, unplanned or unintended conceptions. Every child called to life in this way has the right to grow and to be born. Their conception cannot be regarded as a means to an end, to which only some will be born. Man can never be the means to achieve the goal, because he is the goal of our solidarity and responsibility. The second objective is to restore legal order in the Republic of Poland. Polish law in many legal acts speaks about the conceived child. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 157a The Penal Code includes protection not only of his life but also of his health. Its rights cannot be revoked only to facilitate the operation of clinics, in vitro laboratories. The proposed law corrects in vitro legislation, restoring its consistency with the rights of the child conceived elsewhere. That's why he's wearing this special title. It doesn't establish new rights. It restores children who are conceived in a particular situation, such as the fact of extracorporeal conception, their rights confirmed by the Republic. The proposed amendment does not address the issue of moral debate on the ethical properties of in vitro practice. This is an important issue and requires a serious debate, but from a legal point of view, and therefore the direct responsibilities of the State, the essential in vitro effect is of paramount importance. The life of every conceived child must be protected. The fact that Polish legislation on life protection requires consistency was very clear from one of the legal authorities in the Republic of Poland - Prof. Andrzej Zoll. He said: The law must respond to reality. If we see that there are areas in which code protection is not complete and in relation to people so vulnerable, then we propose changes. And this is not a reaction to one tragic event or another, because so the Penal Code cannot be changed, but a whole situation that has been happening for years. The professor not only talked about this, but also proposed appropriate changes on behalf of the codification committee during the previous coalition of the Sejm. Today, I hope that the postulates of that time will be fulfilled. However, the need for serious treatment of the rights of children conceived out-of-corporeally most clearly reminded the Polish Parliament in its excellent resolution of 21 July 2006 on the financing of research on human embryos and embryonic stem cells in the European Union. I was not in the Chamber at the time, so I could not vote for it, but it was supported by Prime Minister Beata Szydło, Prime Minister Ewa Kopacz, Prime Minister Jarosław Kaczyński and Marshal Grzegorz Schetyn. Over 80% supported her. Members of our House. I will quote from this act expressing the authentic moral consensus of the nation: Instrumental use of the human body and destruction of conceived life is a drastic violation of human rights. It cannot be justified by any circumstances, and these practices must be abandoned, regardless of the intentions for which they are undertaken. The destruction of human embryos is contrary to the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal interpreting the article. 38 of the Constitution, violates Article. 157a The Penal Code provides for the legal protection of the health of the conceived child and is not acceptable in the light of Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 and 3 of the Code of Medical Ethics. Unfortunately, in later years, disregarding these legal standards, the issue of in vitro debate was as if the people affected by these standards were the most, i.e. children conceived outside the system had no rights already confirmed by the Polish State. Today is the time for us to repeat together what we were able to say 10 years ago. The bill I'm presenting today has come across with the resentment of those who, in vitro, look at technology rather than the situation in which a man begins to live. Of course, every technical argument will be taken seriously in the work. If it has been claimed that the acceptable storage time prior to implantation is 24 hours too short, there is no obstacle to prolonging it in this or even a slightly larger dimension. That's not the point of the problem. The essence is that every child conceived in this way should have the right to continue to develop so that none is destroyed or frozen indefinitely without any real hope of development. Finally, which makes special sense today, I would like to recall the monumental ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal of 28 May 1997 clearly stating that the right to life is protected, including in the prenatal phase, and that life in a democratic state of law must remain under constitutional protection at every stage of its development. That's what I'm talking about. Today it's time to execute the ruling. He's been waiting 20 years for this. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Please refer the bill presented to the House for further work. [[Applause]]
